Same error. The `With x_prev` issue persists regardless of `--ensure-emp`. The problem is fundamental to how QCP handles the `With` annotation.

Let me think about what the `With` annotation does in QCP. In QCP, `With` declares external parameters that are existentially quantified at the caller site. The syntax `/*@ With x_prev */` means the function takes an additional logical parameter `x_prev`. When calling this function, QCP needs to find a value for `x_prev` from the program state.

For `merge(y, z)`: the Require is `dlistrep_shape(x, x_prev) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)`. When calling `merge(y, z)`:
- x = y (first arg)
- The second arg is z
- QCP needs: `dlistrep_shape(y, x_prev) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)`

Now, in the program state at the call site, QCP should have some ownership of `y`. If `y` is in a `dlistrep_shape(y@pre, 0)`, QCP can infer `x_prev = 0`. But if `y` has been modified (advanced through the list), QCP may not have `dlistrep_shape` for the current `y`.

Actually, the issue might be simpler. Let me check: in the `if (x == 0)` branch, the first call is `t = merge(y, z)`. When x == 0, the program enters the if branch:
```c
if (x == 0) {
    t = merge(y,z);
    return t; 
}
```

Here, y and z haven't been modified. The state has `dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)` from the pre-condition. So for merge, x_prev should be 0 (from dlistrep_shape(y, 0)).

But wait, QCP sees `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` (where y is a program variable) and needs `dlistrep_shape(y, x_prev)`. QCP should be able to match x_prev = 0.

Unless the issue is that QCP stores y as `y@pre` (the symbolic value), and the dlistrep_shape uses `y@pre`, and QCP can't map between the program variable y and the symbolic value y@pre when instantiating the With parameter.

For the invariant entrance: QCP needs to prove `PreState |- Invariant`. Given:
- PreState = data_at(&(t->next), u) * data_at(&(t->prev), 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
- Invariant = data_at(&(t->next), u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)

QCP needs to prove: PreState |- Invariant, which means dropping data_at(&(t->prev), 0). This should be valid by the frame rule.

But QCP might need to explicitly match each predicate. The matching might fail if QCP tries to prove Invariant from PreState by canceling matching predicates. The extra `data_at(&(t->prev), 0)` would be left over, which... should be OK? Actually, in separation logic, `P * Q |- P` is not generally true (you can't just drop Q). You need `P * Q |- P * true` which equals `P * Q |- P` only if Q is pure. In separation logic, spatial predicates can't just be dropped.

So QCP needs the invariant to be PRECISELY the right set of predicates - not too weak (can't drop spatial predicates) and not too strong (can't add non-existent predicates).

This means the invariant must precisely match the pre-state for entrance, and precisely match the iterated state for preservation.

Wait, I'm confused about what data_at QCP creates for field expressions. Let me re-read the SKILL.md:

"SEP -- from diff analysis: ... C field expression in a predicate argument (p->prev, t->next) creates an explicit data_at for that field. An exists variable (p_prev) is a pure symbol with no associated data_at."

So: `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)` creates data_at(&(t->next), struct list*, ...) as PART of the spatial state. This is an explicit declaration of ownership.

And dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) where t and u are program variables (not field expressions) does NOT create data_at for t's fields. The connection between t and u is through the predicate semantics only.

So using `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)` in the invariant should directly give QCP data_at(&(t->next), ...) for writing.

- t->next creates data_at(&(t->next), ..., t_next_val) [just the field value]
- dlistrep_shape(y, 0) owns y's list nodes
- dlistrep_shape(z, 0) owns z's list nodes

At iteration 1 (t = x, t->next = u): 
- &(t->next) = &(x->next) = address of x's next field
- This doesn't overlap with y or z lists

At iteration 2: After inserting y and z nodes, t has advanced. t->next = u (the original second node of x). data_at(&(t->next), ...) is at the address of t's next field. Since no other predicate owns this field, there's no overlap.

But wait, what about the processed segment? If I don't include x-t in the invariant, those nodes are lost. But for recording points generation, QCP can drop them (they go into the frame). The recording points would only show what's in the invariant + frame.

For the first iteration, the frame would contain the initial state minus the invariant:
Frame = (dlistrep_shape(x, 0) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)) - (dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0))

= dlistrep_shape(x, 0) - dlistrep_shape(u, x) 
[since t = x, t->next = u, so dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) = dlistrep_shape(u, x)]

dlistrep_shape(x, 0) = data_at(&(x->next), ..., u) * data_at(&(x->prev), ..., 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x)
dlistrep_shape(u, x) = the rest

Frame = data_at(&(x->next), ..., u) * data_at(&(x->prev), ..., 0)

Wait, but the invariant also has data_at(&(t->next), ...) from the field expression. t = x, so data_at(&(x->next), ...). The frame also has data_at(&(x->next), ..., u). This is TWO data_at for the same field: dup_data_at!

So dlistrep_shape(x, 0) in the pre-state provides data_at(&(x->next), ..., u), and the invariant's data_at(&(t->next), ...) = data_at(&(x->next), ...) also provides data_at for x->next. QCP has two owners for the same field. DUP.

This is the fundamental issue with using field expressions in the invariant when the same field is already owned by a pre-state predicate.

To avoid dup, the invariant should NOT include field expressions that overlap with pre-state predicates. But we need data_at for t->next for the loop body to work.

The solution: the invariant's data_at should COME FROM the pre-state's data_at, not be newly created. In separation logic, we need QCP to match the pre-state's data_at(&(x->next), ..., u) with the invariant's data_at(&(t->next), ..., u) (since t = x). But they have different values (u vs. u), so they should match.

But QCP uses exact matching. If the pre-state has data_at(&(x->next), struct list*, u) and the invariant has data_at(&(t->next), struct list*, u) where t = x, QCP needs to:
1. Evaluate t (t = x from the C variable)
2. Compute &(t->next) = &(x->next) (which is addr of x->next)
3. Match data_at(addr of x->next, ..., u) with data_at(addr of x->next, ..., u)

If QCP does the evaluation, it should match. But QCP might compare the raw expressions without evaluation, seeing &(t->next) and &(x->next) as different expressions.

I suspect this is exactly what happens - QCP doesn't fully evaluate field expressions when matching predicates, leading to false dup_data_at errors.

OK, I've spent a lot of time trying to get recording points. Let me try the approach of just going ahead with the invariant I believe is correct, verifying it, and iterating.

Let me try a different strategy entirely. I have RP_pre_loop and RP_after_cond from the while-loop approach. Let me use those along with my code analysis to infer the invariant, then verify it. I'll skip the formal recording point collection.

Based on my analysis:

**Loop purpose**: Interleave nodes from y and z lists into the traversal of x's list. Each full iteration:
1. If y: insert y's head node after t, advance y
2. If z: insert z's head node after t, advance z  
3. Link u after t, advance t to u, advance u

**Key observations**:
- x is the head (never changes)
- t is the current tail of the merged segment
- u = t->next is the next original node to process
- y and z are remaining lists to interleave

**Invariant structure**:
At loop entry:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u): processed segment from x to t, with t->next = u
- dlistrep_shape(u, t): remaining original x list from u
- dlistrep_shape(y, 0): remaining y list
- dlistrep_shape(z, 0): remaining z list

But QCP's strategies can't handle dllseg_shape in the way needed. The SKILL.md's common fix strategies suggest using field expressions for the boundary:

"3. Widen the boundary with field expressions on both sides: If entail_wit_2 or return_wit fail, the boundary between dllseg and dlistrep may be too tight for strategies to fold/unfold. Use field expressions on BOTH sides to explicitly expose the gap node's fields — the boundary becomes self-adapting and no strategy rule is needed at all."

So maybe the correct invariant is:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)
```

Wait, let me re-read strategy 3 from the SKILL.md more carefully:

The example: DLL backward traversal loop (p = p->prev).
Fix: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)`

In this example:
- dllseg_shape uses p->prev and p as field expressions
- dlistrep_shape uses p->next as field expression
- Both are C field expressions that re-evaluate after p changes

Applying this pattern to my forward traversal (interleaving):
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

Wait, the pattern from the SKILL.md is:
```
dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)
```
Which covers: segment head to p->next, last node (end of segment) at p, with p->next as endpoint. Wait no, dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p):
- Start: head, start_prev: 0
- yp (last node in segment): p->prev
- y (endpoint, not in segment): p

And dlistrep_shape(p->next, p): list starting at p->next with prev p.

So p is NOT in the segment (it's the endpoint). p->prev is the last node in the segment. p->next is the start of the remaining list.

The invariant covers: segment from head to p (exclusive) + list from p->next.

But what about p itself? p->prev and p->next are field expressions that create data_at for those fields. The node p IS represented through its fields.

For my loop: the boundary is at t (last processed node) and u (next node). 

Applying the pattern:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(u->next, u)  // NO, this doesn't match
```

Wait, let me think about this differently. In my loop:
- t is the last PROCESSED node (in the merged segment)
- u = t->next is the next UNPROCESSED original node
- The segment covers x to u (exclusive), with t as last node
- The remaining list starts at u

For the boundary to be "self-adapting" with field expressions:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)
```

Here:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next): segment from x to t->next (exclusive), last node = t
  - This means t is IN the segment
  - t->next is the endpoint (NOT in segment)
  - The field expression t->next creates data_at(&(t->next), ...) → direct ownership!
- dlistrep_shape(t->next, t): list from t->next with prev = t
  - The field expression t->next creates data_at(&(t->next), ...) → DUP with dllseg_shape!

Both dllseg_shape and dlistrep_shape reference t->next, so BOTH try to create data_at(&(t->next), ...). This is dup_data_at.

The SKILL.md example avoids dup by using DIFFERENT fields:
- dllseg_shape uses `p->prev` and `p` (creates data_at for p->prev, NOT p->next)
- dlistrep_shape uses `p->next` and `p` (creates data_at for p->next, NOT p->prev)

Each field expression appears in exactly ONE predicate. That's why it avoids dup.

For my loop, I could do:
- One predicate uses t->prev (field expression)
- Another uses t->next (field expression)

The assignment t->next = y needs data_at for t->next. So t->next must be a field expression in at least one predicate. But if only one predicate has t->next, there's no dup.

What if:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(u, t)  // where u is a variable
```

dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next):
- Uses t->next as field expression → data_at(&(t->next), ...)
- Last node in segment = t
- Endpoint = t->next

dlistrep_shape(u, t):
- u is a variable (not field expression)
- No data_at created for u

But for the self-adapting boundary, I need u to adapt when t changes. If u is a variable, it doesn't adapt automatically. After t = u (in the loop body), u is still the old value.

Hmm, this approach doesn't work for forward traversal where t advances.

Actually, wait. Let me re-read the SKILL.md's strategy 3 example more carefully:

"DLL backward traversal loop (p = p->prev). Invariant `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p_prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)` with an exists variable p_prev leaves 1 Admitted. Fix: `dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(p->next, p)` — both arguments are C field expressions that re-evaluate automatically after p = p->prev."

In the fix:
- dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p): the 3rd param (yp, last node) = p->prev. The 4th param (endpoint) = p.
  - p->prev is the last node in the segment
  - p is the endpoint
  - Creates data_at for p->prev (to evaluate the field expression)
- dlistrep_shape(p->next, p): the list starts at p->next with prev = p
  - Creates data_at for p->next

After p = p->prev:
- p changes, so p->prev and p->next change
- Before: p points to node N. p->prev = N->prev, p->next = N->next
- After: p points to node N->prev. p->prev = (N->prev)->prev, p->next = (N->prev)->next = N

The boundaries automatically shift. No strategy rules needed.

For MY loop (forward traversal with interleaving):
- t advances: t = u (at the end of each iteration)
- u advances: u = u->next

What field expressions would auto-adapt?

After t = u:
- t->next = u->next (which is the new u)
- t->prev = u->prev (which was set to t in the loop body)

So the boundary at t->next auto-adapts. And dlistrep_shape starts from t->next.

What about the segment? The segment from x to t:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, ?, t->next)
- The last node is t. But t is not a field expression.
- If I use `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next)`:
  - t is a variable (last node)
  - t->next is a field expression (endpoint)
  - Creates data_at for t->next

After t = u:
- t is now u (new value)
- t->next is now u->next (new value)
- The segment from x to new t->next includes all nodes up to the new u

This might work! Let me try:

```
data_at(&(t->next), struct list *, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

Wait, I already have data_at(&(t->next), ..., u) from this invariant. The issue was that u is a variable, not a field expression. After t = u, the data_at value (u) doesn't change because u is a fixed symbolic value from the invariant's perspective.

What if I use t->next as a field expression instead of u as a variable?

```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

This has t->next in BOTH dllseg_shape AND dlistrep_shape → dup_data_at.

What if I use t in one predicate and t->next in another?
```
data_at(&(t->next), struct list *, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

Hmm, `data_at(&(t->next), ..., t->next)` is circular and doesn't make sense.

OK let me try yet another approach. What if the invariant uses t->next as the first argument of dlistrep_shape (which is fine, it owns from t->next) and also uses dllseg_shape WITHOUT t->next as a field expression?

```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

Here:
- dllseg_shape uses variables t and u (no field expressions)
- dlistrep_shape uses field expression t->next → creates data_at(&(t->next), ...)

No dup because t->next only appears in dlistrep_shape!

But I tried dllseg_shape before and got "Assign Exec fail" because strategies couldn't expose t->next. With dlistrep_shape also creating data_at(&(t->next), ...), QCP has direct access to t->next for writing.

Will this work? At entry, the pre-state has:
- data_at(&(x->next), ..., u) * data_at(&(x->prev), ..., 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)

Invariant (with t = x, u = x->next):
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, x, x->next) + dlistrep_shape(x->next, x)
  = data_at(&(x->next), ...) * data_at(&(x->prev), ...) + dlistrep_shape(u, x)

Wait, dllseg_shape(x, 0, x, u) unfolds to data_at(&(x->next), u) * data_at(&(x->prev), 0). But then dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) = dlistrep_shape(x->next, x) needs to match dlistrep_shape(u, x) from the pre-state.

For forward traversal where t advances to u and u advances to u->next, the self-adapting boundary uses field expressions. Following the SKILL.md pattern:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)
```

Let me try the field expression approach from the SKILL.md example adapted to forward traversal:
```

```

Here:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next): segment from x to t->next (exclusive), last node = t
  - t is the last processed node
  - t->next is the endpoint (NOT in segment)
  - creates data_at(&(t->next), ...) for t->next field expression
- dlistrep_shape(t->next, t): list starting at t->next with prev = t
  - creates data_at(&(t->next), ...) for evaluating t->next

This has t->next as a field expression in BOTH predicates → dup_data_at.

The SKILL.md avoids this by using DIFFERENT fields:
```

```
- p->prev in dllseg (data_at for p->prev)
- p->next in dlistrep (data_at for p->next)
Separate fields → no dup.

- I need t->next in some predicate for writing
- I could use t->prev in the other predicate

- t->next as field expression → data_at for t->next

Both create data_at for t->next → dup.

- Use DIFFERENT field expressions in different predicates to avoid dup
- The boundary auto-adapts when field expressions change

For my loop:
```
dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
```

dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t):
- creates data_at for t->prev
- segment from x to t (exclusive), last = t->prev
- t->prev is the last node IN the segment

dlistrep_shape(t->next, t):
- creates data_at for t->next
- list from t->next with prev = t
- t->next is the START of the remaining list

Total: data_at for t->prev (from dllseg) + data_at for t->next (from dlistrep). Different fields → no dup!

And the ownership covers: 
- dllseg_shape: from x to t->prev (inclusive), with t->prev->next = t
- dlistrep_shape: from t->next (inclusive), with t->next->prev = t

The node t itself: its prev and next are owned by data_at for t->prev and t->next respectively. But t itself as a node is split across two data_at's. Is this valid?

In separation logic, a node's fields can be owned separately if they don't overlap (next and prev are different fields). The data_at for t->next is the next field of t. The data_at for t->prev is the prev field of t. Both are at the same node but different offsets → no overlap.

But the dllseg_shape and dlistrep_shape predicates also own nodes. dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) owns from x to t->prev. dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) owns from t->next. They don't overlap (t->prev and t->next are different nodes, unless t has only one node — but that would mean t is the only node, which doesn't happen in iteration 2+).

Actually, in iteration 2, t could be the ONLY remaining processed node (not including x). But dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) would own x to t->prev, and if t->prev = x, the segment is just x. dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) owns t->next onward. No overlap.

Actually, wait. The loop semantics with invariant I:
```
while (u) /*@ Inv I */ { body }
```

Is translated to:
```
// Enter: Pre |- I
// Loop: I && u |- body; Post; Post |- I'  (preservation)
// After: I && !u |- rest (exit)
```

At preservation: we prove `I && u` before the body, then after the body we have state `Post`, and we prove `Post |- I'`. In `Post`, u might be 0 or non-null.

If I' has `u != 0`, then proving `Post |- I'` requires `Post` to also have `u != 0`. But if u became 0 in the body, `Post` doesn't have `u != 0`, and the proof fails.

   - 4 `entail_wit_2_N` (preservation, 4 branches)
   - 3 `return_wit_N` (postcondition, 3 return paths)

   The invariant covers the loop body's field accesses (t->next, u->prev, y->prev, z->prev) but the processed segment (x to t) is in the frame, making the postcondition and entry entailments unprovable by the automatic prover. Attempts to add dllseg_shape for the processed segment did not reduce the admitted goal count.
